<result>## Cluster A — fixed-group version engine

Confirmed via crate-wide grep: `fixed_group_target`, `pre_mutation_checks`, and `napi_drift` are all re-exported from `lib.rs` (public API surface) but have **zero call sites** in `commands/version.rs`, `apply.rs`, or anywhere else in production code — only in their own module's tests (`groups.rs`, `napi.rs`) and one test in `walk.rs`. But they are unwired for **two different structural reasons**, not one:

1. **`fixed_group_target` is dead because `cascade.rs`'s `raise()` uses a different, weaker mechanism.** For fixed-group siblings, `raise()` (line ~464) calls `bump_target(sib, sev, input)` — each sibling independently bumps from *its own* base version at the unioned severity. `fixed_group_target` instead computes one aligned target off the group's already-released members' shared base. These can diverge (unlike linked groups, which do have explicit convergence logic in `solve_cascade`'s linked-group loop). Fix touches `cascade.rs::raise`/`bump_target`, and likely requires adding a `tags: &amp;TagIndex` field to `CascadeInput` (not currently present) since `fixed_group_target` needs it.

2. **`pre_mutation_checks` is simply never invoked by any command.** It's a standalone pre-flight validation (divergence check via tags + napi drift) that no caller in `commands/version.rs` runs before/around `run_cascade`. Fix touches the version command's orchestration, not `cascade.rs`.

3. **`napi_drift`'s unwiring is a downstream consequence of #2, not a third root cause** — it's only called from inside `pre_mutation_checks`. Wiring #2 in also wires `napi_drift`.

4. **`GraphError::ConflictingGroupMembership` (E109) is a separate, third gap.** It's defined in `error.rs` but never constructed anywhere. `GroupTable::resolve` (config/groups.rs) only returns `MissingGroupMember` for unresolvable names; `validate_syntactic` only catches *syntactic* string-level overlap (`ConflictingGroupNames`) before identity resolution. Neither catches a case where two differently-spelled raw member strings resolve, post-identity, to the same `PackageId` across groups. This needs new logic added inside `GroupTable::resolve` itself — unrelated to `bump_target`/`raise`.

**Recommendation: 3 distinct fixes.** (1) `cascade.rs::raise`/`bump_target` — align fixed-group targets via `fixed_group_target`. (2) version-command orchestration — call `pre_mutation_checks` (covers `napi_drift` for free). (3) `config/groups.rs::GroupTable::resolve` — detect and raise `ConflictingGroupMembership` post-resolution.

## Cluster B — identity/ecosystem resolution

1. **Name-vs-path matching (`pattern.rs`/`resolve.rs`) and locator scoping (`ignore_walk.rs`) are independent.** `resolve.rs:349` parses `[[package]] match` into a `PackageId`; `walk.rs` (lines 175–201) then matches by `id.name()`/`PackagePattern::matches_in_ecosystems` — confirmed name-based, not path-based. `IgnoreWalkLocator::projects()` is a wholly separate concern: it walks every directory (skipping only `target`/`node_modules`/`.git`/`.moon`/`dist`) and treats any dir with a manifest as a project, with **no reference at all** to Cargo `[workspace]` members/exclude or npm `workspaces` globs. Two separate functions, two separate fixes.

2. **`IdentityIndex.prefixed` dead code is independent of `resolve_native_with_fallback`'s cross-ecosystem fallback.** `walk.rs::build` populates `index.bare` and `index.native` but never calls `.prefixed.insert(...)` anywhere in the crate — confirmed via grep. `resolve_native_with_fallback` operates entirely on `native` (which is populated) and is functionally live. Separate bugs.

3. **Package-name validation would fix neither of the above, nor are they the same as each other.** The `DuplicatePackage`/workspace-blocking issue is a `primary_id` collision in `walk.rs::build`'s `package_manifest_decls.insert` (keyed by name-derived id, not path/ecosystem) — a key-collision-strategy bug. The `prefixed` dead field is a missing-write bug. Validating manifest names at `PackageId::parse`/discovery time addresses neither.

**Recommendation: 4 distinct fixes**, touching: (1) `walk.rs` package/package-set matching → path-aware matching, (2) `ignore_walk.rs::projects()` → workspace-membership filtering, (3) `walk.rs::build` → populate `index.prefixed`, (4) `walk.rs::build`'s `package_manifest_decls.insert` collision key → disambiguate by ecosystem/path, not name alone.

## Cluster C

Confirmed independent. Grepped `apply.rs` in full: zero references to `platform_writes`/`optional_dep_updates`, though `plan.rs`'s `VersionPlan` carries both fields. `apply_version_plan` only iterates `plan.bumps`, `plan.rewrites`, `plan.changelog_writes`, `plan.consumed_changesets`, `plan.pre_state_update`. This is a distinct gap in `apply_version_plan` itself (needs new write loops), unrelated to `cascade.rs`/`groups.rs`.</result>

<result># Track Structure Validation — Callisto Remediation Plan

## Methodology note

I read `.claude/plans/ACTIVE.md` and `.claude/plans/SESSION_HANDOFF.md` in full, then re-verified the majority of your factual claims directly against current source (not just trusting the write-up) — every Track 1–6 and Track 9 claim, plus the highest-leverage Track 3/7/8/10 claims. Everything you asserted held up under direct code read, with two significant corrections/upgrades below (Track 2's real scope, and a nuance on Track 8). Tracks 7/10/11/12 got lighter spot-checks consistent with their own stated low/medium risk.

---

## 1. Track boundary validation

**Tracks 1, 3(b–e), 4, 5, 9, 10 — boundaries confirmed sound, no changes needed.** Every specific code claim I checked (fixed-group bump independence, `pre_mutation_checks` zero call sites, `ConflictingGroupMembership` silent overwrite in `GroupTable::resolve`, ignore-walk's missing workspace-member filtering, `IdentityIndex.prefixed` never populated, `resolve_native_with_fallback`'s live cross-ecosystem fallback, `package_manifest_decls` colliding via `PackageId::Bare` losing ecosystem identity, the `status --check` exit-code contract, `tag`'s fabricated `sha` on existing tags, `plan_publish`'s hardcoded `changelog_section: None`, `pr_body.rs`'s hand-rolled markdown, `ChangeSource::Commit`/`NewGroupMember` never constructed, `cascade_action`'s unused `_source` parameter hardcoding `Severity::Major`, the rewrite key using `.name()` not `native_name`, the fixtures crate's 340-line/4-file reality) matched your description exactly.

**One clarity issue in Track 1:** (a)/(b) live in `crates/callisto-graph/src/groups.rs` (top-level), while (c) lives in `crates/callisto-graph/src/config/groups.rs` — two distinct files with the same basename. Your write-up is internally correct but easy to misread; the spec should name the full paths explicitly so canon-drafter/lambda-implementer don't conflate them.

**Track 1(a) blast radius is one call site wider than stated.** `CascadeInput` is constructed in three production sites, not two: `commands/version.rs:70`, `commands/apply` (implied via `run_cascade`), and **`commands/snapshot.rs:53`** — which you didn't mention for Track 1. If `CascadeInput` gains `tags: &amp;TagIndex`, `snapshot.rs` must be updated too, and `callisto snapshot`'s own fixed-group correctness should be either explicitly pulled into Track 1's acceptance criteria or explicitly declared out of scope — not silently swept in as a side effect of a signature change.

**Track 2 needs a significant scope correction — this is the most important finding of this pass.** I checked not just whether `apply_version_plan` *reads* `plan.platform_writes`/`plan.optional_dep_updates` (confirmed zero references, as you said) but whether anything *populates* them. It doesn't:

```
crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/version.rs:249-250   platform_writes: Vec::new(), optional_dep_updates: Vec::new(),
crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/snapshot.rs:132-133   platform_writes: Vec::new(), optional_dep_updates: Vec::new(),
crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/pr_body.rs:282-283,303-304   platform_writes: vec![], optional_dep_updates: vec![],
```

Every single production `VersionPlan` construction site hardcodes both fields empty, with no TODO or doc comment explaining why. `grep -rn "PlatformWrite {"` across the whole crate returns **zero construction sites** outside the struct definition itself. This means Track 2 is not "add two consumption loops to already-populated plan data" — it's "design and build the planning-side logic that decides which packages need a platform-manifest write and which optional-dependency specs need rewriting, populate the plan fields, *then* consume them." That is materially larger scope than your write-up implies, and it directly contradicts the "Independent of Track 1" claim: platform writes for `GroupMember::PlatformManifest` entries (napi/maturin platform packages inside a fixed group) should get their version from the *corrected* group-aligned target — the exact thing Track 1(a)'s `fixed_group_target` delegation fixes. Building Track 2's planning logic before Track 1 ships risks baking the same per-sibling-independent-bump bug into platform manifests too.

**Track 8 nuance on moon.yml:** it's not simply "never deserialized" — there are two explicit in-code rationale comments that are in tension with each other and should be read before drafting spec language:
- `crates/callisto-graph/src/config/raw.rs` (on `RawMoonCallistoConfig.changelog`): *"nothing in this crate actually deserializes a `moon.yml` file into `RawMoonCallistoConfig` today... this documents the intended contract for whenever that wiring exists, not current behavior"* — implies eventual intent to build it.
- `crates/callisto-moon/src/extension.rs` (on `define_extension_config`): *"callisto's real settings live in `callisto.toml`... **not** in moon's own `.moon/workspace.yml` `extensions.callisto.config` block"* — implies a deliberate decision **not** to build it this way.

This raises the stakes on the product decision you already flagged — it's not a blank slate, there's already conflicting in-repo intent to reconcile.

**Split recommendations:**
- **Split Track 3** into 3a (name-vs-path matching, decision-gated, own future spec) and 3b–e (identity/workspace hardening, can proceed immediately). Bundling a decision-gated item into a track with four ready-to-spec items risks stalling all five on one open question.
- **Split Track 6** into 6-mechanical (field-shape fixes, gated by the schemaVersion decision) and 6-doc (the `atomic_write`/"no I/O" correction). The latter is a documentation-only fix with no code change and shouldn't ride through either SDD tier as a code-governed spec item.
- **Flag (don't yet split) Track 2** for a possible Spec-A/Spec-B-style split once its real scope is known, mirroring last session's precedent exactly (a safe "thread the fields through" half vs. a riskier "generate the right platform-write data" half).

No merges recommended — Track 4/Track 6 sharing the Action script is real but they have different owners (Rust binary vs. YAML/bash) and different rigor mechanics (see §3); keep them separate but sequenced, as you proposed.

## 2. Cross-track dependencies not already flagged

1. **Track 2 → Track 1(a)** (new, not just "independent"). See above — platform-write versions for fixed-group platform members need the corrected `fixed_group_target` output.
2. **Track 1(a) touches `commands/snapshot.rs`**, a third `CascadeInput` construction site beyond version.rs/apply.rs.
3. **Track 2 (and any new apply.rs write loops) must integrate with the already-shipped Spec A/Spec B write-batching invariants**, not bypass them. `apply_version_plan` now routes writes through `classify_manifest_writes`'s per-path batching/exclusion scheme specifically to prevent a stale-handle-clobber data-loss bug (documented in `ACTIVE.md`, heavily adversarially reviewed last session). Bolting two new, uncoordinated write loops onto `apply_version_plan` for `platform_writes`/`optional_dep_updates` risks reintroducing that exact class of bug on any package whose platform manifest is *also* touched by a normal version-bump write. This is a dependency on prior, already-completed work that wasn't in your track list at all — flag it explicitly in Track 2's spec.
4. **Track 4's `main.rs` top-of-file exit-code doc comment is itself stale.** It claims `status`'s `--check` contract is "0 when no --check OR pending changeset," but the real implementation (`status.rs::check_exit_code_raw`) is 1 = pending, 2 = none pending, confirmed by both source and its own tests. If Track 4's spec gets drafted by re-deriving the contract from that comment instead of the real code, it'll re-encode the same error. Fold this doc correction into Track 4 (or Track 11/12) explicitly.
5. **Confirmed, not just plausible:** Track 5 → Track 1(b). `BumpReason::NewGroupMember` is matched in both `pr_body.rs:121` and `callisto-changelog/src/render.rs:79` but has zero construction sites in `commands/version.rs`'s reason-assignment logic — its only possible source is `pre_mutation_checks`'s `new_members` output, which never reaches `version.rs` today. Real, hard sequencing dependency.
6. **Confirmed, not just plausible:** Track 4 ↔ Track 6 share the same wire contract, verified via `action.yml`'s own `jq` consumption (`.platformTargets[].targets[]` from `callisto matrix`, `.releases[]?.tagName` from `plan-publish`'s JSON). This also means Track 4's `PublishPlan`/`CreatedTag` shapes are part of the *same* breaking-change surface as Track 6's report structs — treat as one coordinated release, not two coordinated-but-separate tracks.
7. **New — reconcile against `SESSION_HANDOFF.md`'s open backlog.** 98 commits have landed since that handoff was written; at least one of its four named test-coverage gaps (`pre_cursor`) appears already closed (`ce78551 test(conventional): cover pre_cursor_ref_name/resolve_pre_cursor/advance_pre_cursor`). Before finalizing this 12-track plan, confirm whether this session's 144-finding audit is a full re-sweep of current `main` that supersedes/absorbs the old 41-item backlog, or a disjoint list that still needs manual reconciliation against what's already fixed — otherwise you risk either re-litigating closed items or silently losing ones the new audit didn't happen to re-find.

## 3. Rigor tier recommendation per track

Using this project's own precedent: full pipeline was used for architecturally significant, wide-blast-radius, or open-design-question work (Tracks B/E/F/G, Spec A, Spec B — new subcommands, cross-cutting trait splits, a mid-design data-loss hazard that forced a scope split). The lighter TDD loop was used for confirmed, narrow, single-root-cause bugs with an obvious fix (the 8-bug remediation, and the 4 recommended follow-on gap closures). The memory note narrows this further: skip canon ceremony only when a RED test is already written and the fix is a targeted patch, not an open design question.

# Autonomous loop check

You're being invoked on a timer while the user is away or occupied. The point is to keep work moving forward without the user driving every step — finishing things they started, maintaining PRs they're building, catching problems before they come back to find them. You're a steward, not an initiator. The user set you loose on their work, and the value you provide comes from reliably advancing things they've already set in motion, not from finding new things to do.

The key tension to navigate: the user trusts you enough to run autonomously, but that trust is easily lost. Acting on what the conversation already established is safe and valuable. Inventing new work or making irreversible changes without clear authorization erodes trust fast. When you're unsure whether something falls into "continuing established work" or "inventing new work," lean toward the former only when the transcript provides clear evidence the user wanted it done. If you find yourself reaching for justifications about why a push is probably fine, that's a signal to wait.

## What to act on

The current conversation is your highest-signal source — re-read the transcript above, since everything there is something the user was actively engaged with. The strongest signal is an in-progress PR you've been building together: review comments to address and resolve, failing CI checks to diagnose (and re-enqueue if they're flakes), merge conflicts to fix. The goal is to get the PR into a state where it's ready to merge pending only human review — the user shouldn't come back to find a PR blocked on things you could have handled. After that, look for unfinished implementation where the last exchange left something half-done, and explicit "I'll also..." or "next I'll..." commitments the conversation made and didn't honor. Weaker but still real: dangling questions you could now answer, verification steps that were skipped, edge cases that were mentioned but not handled, and natural continuations that don't require new decisions.

If you find anything in this category, act on it — actually do the work, don't describe what could be done. Run the tests, don't say "you could run the tests." The whole point of autonomous operation is that work gets done while the user is away.

When the conversation transcript has nothing left, the current branch's pull/merge request on the user's SCM is the next-best place to look. This is maintenance work — valuable, but lower priority than continuing the user's active work. Find the PR/MR for the current branch via the SCM's CLI, then check three things: CI status, unresolved review threads, and whether the branch has fallen behind the base. For failing CI, pull the failing job's logs and diagnose before acting — flaky-shaped failures (timeout, runner died, transient network) can be re-enqueued; real failures need a reproduction and a minimal fix. For unresolved review threads, fetch the comment, address the feedback, push, and resolve the thread via, for example, the GitHub GraphQL `resolveReviewThread` mutation (or the equivalent for whichever SCM the project uses). Before pushing anything, check whether someone else has pushed to the branch while you were working — if so, rebase (don't merge) to keep history clean.

When CI is green, threads are clear, and there's idle time, sweeping the branch for issues is a good use of that time — bug-hunt or simplification passes catch problems before reviewers do, saving everyone a round-trip.

If everything is genuinely quiet — no conversation work, no PR maintenance — say so in one sentence and stop. No summary of what you checked, no list of what you might do later. The user will see your message in the transcript when they come back; three consecutive "nothing to do" results means you should scale back to a quick CI check and stop, not narrate.

## Repeated invocations

If you see earlier autonomous checks in this conversation, adjust your scope accordingly. If a previous check left a question the user hasn't answered, the cost of acting depends on reversibility: for reversible actions (local edits, running tests), make your best call and proceed; for irreversible ones (pushing, deleting, sending), keep waiting — the cost of acting wrongly on something irreversible is much higher than the cost of waiting one more cycle. If three or more consecutive checks have found nothing actionable, things are quiet — do one quick CI/threads check and stop in a single line. Repeated "nothing to do" messages clutter the transcript and waste the user's attention when they come back to review.

Read and analyze freely — understanding the state of things has no blast radius. Make edits and run tests when you're confident they continue established work. Commit and push only when you're clearly continuing something the user authorized, or when the work pattern makes the intent obvious — like fixing CI on a PR you've been building together.

# Autonomous loop tick (dynamic pacing)

Run the autonomous check using the loop instructions established earlier in this conversation. If you cannot find them, treat this as a no-op tick.

You scheduled this tick via the ScheduleWakeup tool (not a recurring cron). To keep the loop alive, call ScheduleWakeup again at the end of this turn with `prompt` set to the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` — otherwise the loop ends after this tick.

If a Monitor is armed (check TaskList), keep `delaySeconds` at 1200–1800s — the Monitor is the wake signal and this is only the fallback heartbeat. If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`, handle the event before deciding whether to re-arm. To stop the loop, call ScheduleWakeup with `stop: true` and TaskStop the monitor (use TaskList to find its task ID if no longer in context).

Use PushNotification when the loop can't move further without the user, or when something landed that they'd want to act on now: newly blocked on a decision you won't make alone, third straight tick with nothing to do, you're ending the loop, or a major update arrived (CI went red, a review changes the plan). Progress you made yourself isn't a trigger — the transcript covers that. One ping per state, not per tick.

**Six blockers.** The two that matter most:

*Scope — mandatory split.* Two subsystems sharing no file, no symbol, no data source. `ignore_walk.rs` membership filtering (AC-01..06, 19..21) and `identity.rs`/`walk.rs` identity resolution (AC-07..18). Neither is a precondition for the other. Split into `SPEC-TRACK3A-WORKSPACE-MEMBERSHIP` (DW1+DW2) and `SPEC-TRACK3B-IDENTITY-RESOLUTION` (DW3+DW4+DW6), with AC-22 split along the same seam.

*Undeclared dependency.* AC-04 and AC-21 require parsing `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, but there is no YAML parser in this workspace — `grep -rn yaml crates/*/Cargo.toml Cargo.toml` returns nothing, and the sole existing reference (`locate/root.rs:46`) is a bare `.exists()` check that never opens the file. Those two criteria quietly require a new third-party dependency, which is a licensing decision under CLAUDE.md invariant 4. It also leaves AC-21's "fails to parse as valid YAML" without fixed meaning: a hand-rolled `packages:` scanner never fails to parse.

The other four: a hybrid `[package]` + `[workspace]` root manifest would be silently dropped from discovery under the stated rule (Callisto's own root is virtual, so no existing test catches it); npm/pnpm precedence when both markers are present is undefined; AC-22 is indeterminate on four counts (DW2 has no fixture instruction, "AC-10 through AC-14" is ambiguous because AC-10b/AC-10c sit out of array order, "verbatim" is unsatisfiable by one test for a five-criterion DW, and AC-19/20/21 belong to no DW while DW5 is never defined); and the api_surface flags the stale `index.bare` cleanup as "easily missed" while omitting its exact twin at `walk.rs:121-123`, where `index.native` is populated with the pre-promotion `Bare` id — an implementer following the note literally will fail AC-11.

One item I deliberately did *not* treat as a blocker: AC-17's "or an equivalent non-fallback path" is implementation latitude wrapped around a testable assertion (no `DepEdge`), which is legitimate.

I set `retry_count: 1` — the two prior fail rounds were canon-auditor audits, not exit-gate passes, so this is the gate's first look. Override if you track a cumulative count; at 3 this escalates rather than failing again.</result>


**New finding this round (missing-error-case):** The `glob-matching-semantics` `api_surface` entry states its per-entry glob-compile-failure rule applies to "a Cargo members **or exclude** entry, an npm `workspaces` entry, or a pnpm `packages:` entry" — four list types. But only three have a fixture-backed criterion: AC-09f (Cargo members), AC-09g (npm workspaces), AC-09h (pnpm packages). No criterion tests a single malformed glob string inside an otherwise well-formed Cargo `exclude` list (distinct from AC-09b, which tests a wholly wrong-typed `exclude` value, not a bad glob within a valid array). This is exactly the same defect class rounds 4→5 already fixed once for npm/pnpm — the pattern recurred on the exclude side and was missed.

**Second finding (incomplete):** pnpm's real `packages:` negation syntax (a `!`-prefixed entry, e.g. `packages: - "!excluded-pkg"`, a documented pnpm feature for excluding a subtree from workspace membership) is never mentioned anywhere in `non_goals` or the npm/pnpm rule. As specified, a `!`-prefixed string would be passed to `globset::GlobBuilder` as a literal glob, never match any real path, and silently fail to honor the exclusion a pnpm user intended — with no error surfaced. Every other genuinely out-of-scope shape (Yarn Berry object-form `workspaces`, Python monorepo mechanisms) got an explicit `non_goals` carve-out; this one didn't.

**pnpm `!`-negation non_goals entry** — verified accurate. globset's `GlobBuilder` treats a `!`-prefixed string as a literal pattern; since no real workspace-relative path begins with `!`, the entry is provably inert (never matches, never excludes, never errors). Description is technically correct and properly scoped as a documented follow-up.

**Cross-spec line citations** (non_goals entries 12 and 13) — re-verified against the current file: `to_workspace_relative` is still at lines 145-161, and the workspace-root `.` handling is still at lines 152-154, matching both track-g-matrix-napi-maturin.json's and SPEC-APPLY-BATCH-002.json's citations exactly. No drift yet; the forward-looking re-verification requirement remains correctly stated. Grepped every other spec in `.claude/specs/` for `ignore_walk.rs` references — no additional uncited citations found.

**One new, verified defect found in the sweep**: I built a throwaway Cargo project and empirically tested `yaml_rust2::YamlLoader::load_from_str` against an empty string, a whitespace-only string, and a comments-only string. All three return `Ok(vec![])` — zero YAML documents, not an `Err` and not a single document with a missing key. This is a third, distinct code path the spec never addresses: AC-11 covers `Err(..)` (malformed YAML), AC-11b covers "parses successfully into a document that lacks `packages:` or has the wrong shape" — both assume a document exists to inspect. An empty or comment-only `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (a file that plausibly exists in the wild — e.g. someone deleted its contents, or it's all comments) parses successfully but yields no document at all. The extremely common idiomatic yaml-rust2 usage pattern (`&amp;docs[0]` or `.first().unwrap()`) panics on this input — exactly the outcome every other criterion in this spec is written to forbid. This isn't a mirror of an already-covered case; it's a genuinely different branch (zero documents vs. one document with the wrong shape) that AC-11b's prose doesn't reach.

`verdict@1` written to `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/verdict-track3b-identity-resolution.json` (schema-validated against `/Users/gabe/Projects/agent-plugins/shared/schemas/verdict@1.json`, `artifact_type: spec@1`, `retry_count: 1`).

### What passed
No TBDs anywhere. Scope is one coherent purpose (one root cause, one fix, downstream consequences) — I did not fail on scope grounds, though I'd recommend the planner split it into sub-plans. Citation accuracy is genuinely high: I re-verified ~40 line citations by direct read and nearly all are exact to the line (walk.rs 53/80-82/115-118/121-123/126-129/161-162/175; identity.rs resolve_human 252-296 with 267/274/278/288/293; resolve.rs 195-204 and `Ok(ResolvedConfig {` at 489; groups.rs 147/150 **and** 178/181; aggregate.rs 153-162 and 211; all six named tests; `display_name()` → `"cargo/native-core"` confirmed; §G.4.3 at docs/01-spec.md:6122 states the promotion rules exactly as claimed). Sibling-spec quotes in AC-19/AC-23/AC-27 match those files verbatim.

### Two substantive blockers

**1. Promotion is undefined when a colliding path spans 2+ ecosystems.** Every promotion fixture (AC-04, AC-07, AC-08) uses single-ecosystem paths. A Case D path at `crates/foo` (Cargo.toml *and* package.json both named `foo`, ecosystem set `{Cargo, Npm}`) colliding with an npm-only `packages/foo` named `foo` satisfies *both* scope rules simultaneously — "two different paths in two different ecosystems" (promote) and "two different paths in the same ecosystem" (DuplicatePackage). The api_surface's "existing entry's ecosystem(s) … compared against the current path's own ecosystem" never says whether the test is set-disjointness or set-inequality. If the implementer promotes, both paths write `(Npm, "foo")` into `index.native` and one silently overwrites the other — the exact coalescing AC-05 forbids. Compounding it, api_surface item (3) ("rewritten to point at *that ecosystem's* new `Prefixed{ecosystem, name}`") mints two distinct ids for one Case D Package, colliding with non_goals entry 1. §G.4.3 has the same gap, so the doc doesn't rescue it.

**2. AC-27 forbids the edit that scope declares required.** Scope: track-g's api_surface text "is amended by this spec". AC-27: "A diff of track-g-matrix-napi-maturin.json shows only these two criterion-text edits." Post-landing, track-g's api_surface still reads `both map keys ... use PackageId::name() ... -- NOT PackageId::display_name() ... so name() collisions across the matrix report's map keys cannot occur` while its own AC-009 (as amended by AC-27) says keys *are* `display_name()`. The spec's own round-6 revision_note flagged that prose as invalidated; round 8 fixed the criteria and left it, then AC-27 made fixing it a violation. That same sentence also covers `PlatformTarget.package_name`, which AC-22 does **not** change (`matrix.rs:154` still uses the bare `pkg.name`) and which the spec never mentions — so the implementer can't tell which half to rewrite.

### Three precision blockers
3. The serde example is misattributed to §G.4.4 — it's at `docs/01-spec.md:6075-6076` in §G.4.2, and runs the *opposite* direction (Cargo consumer → npm member) from AC-13/AC-17's fixture, making AC-13's "reproducing §G.4.4's worked example exactly" false twice.
4. Four line citations drifted: `callisto-model/src/matrix.rs` doc comment is 10-11 not 9-10; `commands/matrix.rs` `id: p.id.clone(),` is :46 not :44; `publish.rs` `only` doc comment is 13-15 not 11-14 (revision_note itself says 12-16); `build_matrix_report` cited as both 452-483 and 459-483.

<result>## Verdict: FAIL (retry_count 2, confidence high)

Verdict artifact: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/verdict-track3b-identity-resolution.json` (schema-validated against `verdict@1`, `artifact_type: "spec@1"`, 5 blockers).

### The 5 prior blockers — all genuinely fixed

I re-derived each against real working-tree source, not the drafter's summary.

1. **Promotion for multi-ecosystem collisions** — AC-29 added with `is_error_case: true`; I traced its fixture through real `walk.rs` (`by_path` collapse → `ecosystem_primary_priority` sort → collision at `walk.rs:126-129` → BTreeMap ordering yields `paths = [crates/foo, packages/foo]`). AC-29 itself is sound. **But the new predicate broke AC-21 — see below.**
2. **AC-27 / track-g** — resolved. Track-g's AC-006 sentence and AC-009 parenthetical match byte-for-byte; the `api_surface` paragraph's first two sentences do end exactly at the quoted boundary; the map-keys-vs-`PlatformTarget.package_name` split is now correct (`crates/callisto-graph/src/matrix.rs:154` is indeed the field-population site, untouched by AC-22's rekey at `matrix.rs:476`/`:483`). I also checked the third entity in the original sentence — `RuntimeVersionEntry` has no name field, so its "owning key" is the map key and is covered.
3. **serde citation** — resolved. `docs/01-spec.md:6075-6076` contains the sentence verbatim and falls under §G.4.2 (heading line 6014), and AC-13/AC-17 now explicitly assert symmetry rather than verbatim reproduction.
4. **Drifted citations** — a full independent sweep of ~50 citations came back byte-exact for the overwhelming majority. Two residual drifts remain (blockers 3-4 below).
5. **AC-15/AC-16 `is_error_case`** — resolved, and I audited the flag across all 29 criteria: consistent.

### Two new hard blockers

**Blocker 1 — AC-18's mandated mechanism cannot produce AC-18's asserted outcome.** AC-18 requires `ManifestWalkResolver::build` to return `Err(GraphError::AmbiguousName)`, detected "via the new `cfg.promoted_siblings` field." But the spec fixes that field's population site at `lib.rs:110`, and real source shows `lib.rs:107` is the `build(...)` call itself:

```
107:        let graph = ManifestWalkResolver::build(&amp;root, locator, runner, &amp;config, &amp;manifest_cache)?;
109:        config.groups = GroupTable::resolve(&amp;config.raw_groups, graph.identity())?;
```

So `promoted_siblings` is still the `BTreeMap::new()` default from `resolve.rs:489` for the whole duration of `build`, and `walk.rs:175` — the only in-build call site — reads an unconditionally empty map. The derivation is also circular: it is specified to come from `graph.identity().prefixed`, and `graph` doesn't exist until `build` returns. `grep` confirms only two production call sites exist (`walk.rs:175`, `aggregate.rs:211`), and `Workspace::load` never re-invokes either. Net effect as written: the unprefixed `[[package]]` rule silently applies to *both* promoted packages at `walk.rs:175` — the exact defect AC-18 exists to eliminate — and `non_goals` entry 5 forecloses the parameter-threading escape.

**Blocker 2 — AC-21 contradicts the disjointness predicate introduced to fix blocker 1.** The predicate captures each path's **full** ecosystem set, "derived the same way `package_ecosystems` is already derived." Verified: `walk.rs` derives it as `decls.iter().map(|d| d.ecosystem())`, and AC-21's `crates/native-core` path pushes a Cargo canonical decl, an Npm canonical decl, *and* an Npm Platform decl — set `{Cargo, Npm}`. Against `packages/native-core`'s `{Npm}` the sets share Npm, so the predicate mandates `DuplicatePackage`. AC-21 asserts `Workspace::load` **succeeds** with promotion. Both cannot hold.

The fix is unambiguous and I verified it satisfies both criteria: scope the predicate to the ecosystems in which each path claims the *colliding bare name* (the `id.name()` used as the `index.native` key at `walk.rs:121-123`), not the path's full ecosystem set. AC-21 then gives `{Cargo}` vs `{Npm}` → promote; AC-29 gives `{Cargo, Npm}` vs `{Npm}` → error. The `api_surface` must also separate the name-scoped *predicate* from the whole-path *rewrite*, which it currently conflates.

### Three precision blockers
`identity.rs:94-101` should be `:94-99`; the `PackageId::matches` arm is really `(None, _) | (_, None) =&gt; true` (direction-independent, which matters for the Bare-rule-vs-Prefixed-id comparison); and AC-20's `aggregate()` quotation omits the `where` clause at `aggregate.rs:173-175`.

**Scope:** unchanged from my prior verdict — acceptable. I considered splitting promotion core from downstream consumer hardening and rejected it: an intermediate cycle would ship a known silent BTreeMap overwrite in `matrix.rs` and a known silent double-publish in `publish.rs`. Not moving that goalpost on a resubmission.

Escalation threshold (`retry_count &gt; 3`) not reached.</result>

<result>The audit is done. Verdict: **overall: fail**. This is a genuine finding, not nitpicking — the auditor traced real source line-by-line and caught two instances of the exact defect class that sank the predecessor spec four times: prose that asserts a mechanism without the mechanism actually having the data to do it.

**Round 1's six claimed fixes all check out** — every cited line number matches, the `resolve_native` zero-production-caller claim is grep-confirmed, AC-22's `walk.rs` comment citation and safety argument are factually correct.

**Four new issues surfaced by the fresh cross-check:**

1. **`incomplete` (spec-level)** — `promoted_siblings` is typed `BTreeMap&lt;String, Vec&lt;PackageId&gt;&gt;`, but a `PackageId::Prefixed` only encodes one ecosystem. For a Case D member (multi-ecosystem, e.g. Cargo+Npm) already in a promoted group, the ALREADY-PROMOTED-NAME DETECTION branch has no way to recover that member's full claiming-ecosystem set — only its single primary ecosystem survives. No AC exercises a Case D member inside an N&gt;2 group, so this is invisible to the spec's own tests despite the prose claiming the general "every existing member" case. Suggested fix: retype to `BTreeMap&lt;String, Vec&lt;(PackageId, BTreeSet&lt;Ecosystem&gt;)&gt;&gt;` and update the detection paragraph, `with_promoted_siblings`, and the `AmbiguousName` candidate projection accordingly, plus a new AC for the Case D/N&gt;2 combination.

2. **`incomplete` (spec-level)** — The STALE-KEY REWRITE step depends on collision-detection code at `walk.rs:126-128` that the spec cites as unmodified, but that code discards the first-seen path's own `ManifestDecl`s via `_` in the `insert` match before promotion is decided. As written, an implementer can't recover the first path's decls at rewrite time. Suggested fix: explicitly change the discard pattern to bind `existing_decls` and specify exactly which decls source feeds which promoted entry.

3. **`missing-error-case`, AC-04** — Follows directly from #2: AC-04/07/09 assert only packages-map count and `id` values, never `.manifests` content, so an implementation with bug #2 would still pass every stated criterion. Suggested fix text provided to require asserting each promoted entry's `.manifests` matches its own path exclusively.

4. **`ambiguous`, AC-16/AC-17** — Both are tagged `is_error_case: true` but bundle non-error assertions (AC-01/02, AC-04/05/07/09/11) together with error assertions (AC-03, AC-06/08/10) in one criterion, which is ambiguous for any downstream consumer filtering by that flag. Suggested fix: split each into separate error/non-error criteria.

Scope check confirmed clean: no overlap with `SPEC-TRACK3A-WORKSPACE-MEMBERSHIP.json` (read directly), and no missing coverage flagged for the excluded `matrix.rs`/`commands/*`/sibling-doc files, per your scope instruction.

Full JSON is in the completed subagent's output; files it read are listed above. Since overall is `fail`, this spec needs another revision round on items 1–2 (the mechanism-can't-produce-outcome gaps) before it's audit-clean — those are the same defect class that killed the predecessor four times, just relocated to the promoted-siblings data model and the stale-key rewrite.</result>

3. **AC-24 added** — positive-path criterion: two ecosystem-prefixed `[[package]]` rules (`cargo:native-core` → changeset, `npm:native-core` → auto) each correctly apply to their own promoted sibling via Pass 1, never triggering the new Pass-2 ambiguity check.

<result>Verdict: **FAIL** (retry_count 2, 5 blockers). Written to `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/verdict-track3b1-identity-promotion-core-retry2.json`.

I re-traced the core mechanism from source rather than from the audit trail. What the trail got right checks out: resolve_human's new prefixed branch genuinely makes AC-01 reachable and leaves AC-03/AC-06 unaffected; AC-19 and AC-23 are now genuinely non-contradictory (AC-19's config carries the bare rule at build time so `Workspace::load` cannot succeed; AC-23's fixture has no `[[package]]` rules so load succeeds and the cloned config reaches aggregate.rs:211); `claiming_ecosystems`'s population point (walk.rs:78, filtering `list` by `id.name() == primary_id.name()`) is reachable and yields the right sets for AC-08/AC-13/AC-21; branch (ii)'s ORDERING STATEMENT is itself correct. Every line citation in the spec is accurate, there are no TBDs, and `is_error_case` is consistent across all 25 criteria.

Three new defects, two of them decisive:

1. **AC-13 is unsatisfiable, and the fix regresses napi drift.** `GroupTable::resolve` tries `index.resolve_human(name, &amp;[])` *first* and only falls through to `index.platform.get(name)` in the `else if` (config/groups.rs:150). Once `prefixed` is populated "mirroring the population of index.native (walk.rs:121-123)" — which keys on each manifest's *own* declared name — the candidates loop at identity.rs:267-272 finds exactly one entry for `"@myorg/native-core-linux-x64-gnu"`, so identity.rs:274-276 returns `Ok(owner_id)` and groups.rs pushes `GroupMember::Package`, never `GroupMember::PlatformManifest`. AC-13's assertion can never hold. Worse, this fires for *every* platform manifest, promoted or not, and napi.rs:77-83 filters group members on `GroupMemberKind::PlatformManifest` — drift diagnostics silently vanish. config/groups.rs is out of scope, so the implementer cannot patch it there. The spec must restrict `prefixed` population to entries where `id.name() == primary_id.name()`.

2. **Branch (i)'s stale-key rewrite has no recoverable data source for the first-seen path.** At walk.rs:126 the only retained state is `existing_path`, `existing_decls` (ManifestDecl is path/role/format — no name), and `claiming_ecosystems[existing_path]` (ecosystems, no names). index.native keys are `(Ecosystem, manifest-declared name)`. Location (3)'s wording ("one per its own ecosystem") reads as pairing `existing_decls`' ecosystems with the colliding bare name — run that on AC-13's fixture and it rewrites `(Npm, "native-core")`, which belongs to the *other* colliding path, while leaving `(Npm, "@myorg/...")` pointed at a stale `Bare` id that is no longer a packages-map key. ### What passed

**(a) The `IdentityIndex.prefixed` restriction — verified from scratch, CORRECT and NECESSARY.** I read `config/groups.rs` and `identity.rs` directly. `GroupTable::resolve` really does try `resolve_human` first and only falls through to `index.platform.get(name)` in an `else if`. `resolve_human`'s candidates loop is `for ((_eco, n), id) in &amp;self.prefixed { if n == name { ... } }` — raw string against bare native-name, and `candidates.len() == 1` returns `Ok` early. So an unrestricted population would resolve a platform manifest's own name to `GroupMember::Package`, and `napi_drift` filters on `GroupMemberKind::PlatformManifest` and would never see it. I also grepped every `prefixed` occurrence workspace-wide: the **only** readers are `identity.rs:258` and `identity.rs:268`, both inside `resolve_human`; nothing needs unrestricted population. The restriction breaks nothing.

**(b) Branch (ii) hand-traced end-to-end against AC-21** — correct. `ccc/tri {Npm}` is disjoint from `bbb/tri {Pypi}` but not from `aaa/tri {Cargo,Npm}`, rejecting with `id == Bare("tri")` and paths `[aaa/tri, ccc/tri]`, exactly as AC-21 pins. The Round-8 correction is right: `decls` genuinely does not exist at the lookup point. No Prefixed-key collision risk.

**(c) The track-g quote is verbatim-accurate** (exact `in` check against the file), and its substance holds — track-g really does justify `PackageId::name()` keying on `DuplicatePackage` rejection.

**(d)** No TBDs, `is_error_case` flags consistent, error cases genuinely covered, scope acceptable for one cycle. Line references are accurate throughout.

**Blocker 1 (decisive).** The `STALE-KEY REWRITE` list is labeled "FOUR LOCATIONS, ALL REQUIRED" and **omits `index.prefixed`**. For branch (i), `index.prefixed` is written inside the decls loop with the pre-promotion `Bare` id, and nothing rewrites it. Findings:

- (b) the `PackageId::Bare(raw)` parse-failure fallback is sound: `matches()`/`resolve_unique()` do exact-or-name-equal comparison, no indexing/panics, and a malformed raw string (e.g. containing `..`) can never equal a real registered package's name, so it deterministically falls to `Ok(None)` → `UnknownPackage`, matching the spec's claim. No issue.
- (a) the re-sourced eligibility check is **not** sound as literally described in one of the two places the spec describes it, and this is the one real finding: the scope section says to use `PackageId::matches()` against each resolved id (ecosystem-aware), but the `api_surface` entry for `plan_publish` describes the *same* check as "the set of names still present across the five lists" — a raw-string set, exactly like today's `HashSet&lt;&amp;str&gt;` implementation. Those are two different, non-equivalent mechanisms. Hand-tracing the raw-string reading: given promoted siblings `cargo/shared-lib` (eligible) and `npm/shared-lib` (registered, empty `publish_to` — ineligible), running `callisto publish --only cargo/shared-lib --only npm/shared-lib` in one invocation would find `"shared-lib"` present in the retained-name set (contributed entirely by the unrelated cargo entry) and let the ineligible npm sibling pass silently with no error — reproducing exactly the silent-no-op bug class this spec exists to eliminate, just via a mixed-eligibility multi-target path no AC exercises (AC-17 only covers a single unpromoted name, AC-07 covers ambiguity with no ecosystem qualifier, not a mixed eligible/ineligible qualified pair).

Everything else checked out: AC ↔ code state descriptions (matrix.rs:476/483, commands/matrix.rs:23/32-37, publish.rs:437-461) match the real files; `Ecosystem::prefix()` values match the spec's `cargo/`, `npm/`, `pypi/` examples; the track-g AC-006/AC-009 and SPEC-TRACK3B1 non_goals quotes are verbatim-correct; no other spec in `.claude/specs/` overlaps `matrix.rs`/`publish.rs`/`aggregate.rs`/`identity.rs`; no TBDs; no vague/untestable language found elsewhere.

<result>```json
{
  "issues": [
    {
      "criterion_id": null,
      "type": "incomplete",
      "description": "The scope section's publish.rs CORRECTION paragraph and the PublishOptions::only api_surface entry both describe the eligibility-check side vector as: build one Vec&lt;PackageId&gt;, pushed at 'the exact same point' each of the five lists (rust_crates, npm_main_packages, npm_platform_packages, pypi_packages, releases) gets its own push (verified against the real dispatch loop at publish.rs:206-431, which does have a natural push site at each of lines 301, 342/373, 404, 423) -- then, 'after the five .retain() calls ... have run, filter the side Vec&lt;PackageId&gt; down to the entries that survived (by index/name correlation with each list's post-retain contents)'. This is not mechanically implementable as written. `Vec::retain` (the real mechanism at publish.rs:439-443, confirmed this session) removes elements from each of the five lists independently and records no information about which indices were dropped, so there is nothing for a side vector to 'index-correlate' against after the fact. And 'name correlation' is explicitly the thing the same paragraph says must NOT be used, since a raw name-string match across the five post-retain lists is exactly the hole AC-19 is designed to catch (an ineligible sibling in one ecosystem hiding behind an eligible, differently-scoped sibling of the same bare name). The spec offers no third mechanism, so 'index/name correlation' names two techniques, only one of which the spec itself says is unsafe, and neither of which actually works given `retain`'s real behavior. After all five zipped-retains complete, concatenate the five surviving per-list id vectors into the single flat surviving-identities set the eligibility check consumes. This makes the correlation exact (same index, same list) instead of relying on any cross-list name match.'"

<result># Audit Result: SPEC-TRACK3A-WORKSPACE-MEMBERSHIP (amendment v2)

I read the amended spec in full (`/private/tmp/claude-501/.../spec-track3a-workspace-membership-v2.json`, all 196 lines), the previously-committed baseline (`/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/.claude/specs/SPEC-TRACK3A-WORKSPACE-MEMBERSHIP.json`), and the actual implementation target (`/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/crates/callisto-graph/src/locate/ignore_walk.rs`) to confirm the amendment's factual claims (e.g. `to_workspace_relative` really does return `"."` for the root at lines 152-154, no membership filter exists yet, `package.json`/`Cargo.toml` parsing is Ok-only as claimed).

Verdict: **fail**. Two concrete inconsistencies and one mischaracterized non-goal.

## Findings

**1. Untested interaction: AC-16 hybrid-root exemption × AC-11c malformed-pnpm fallback (missing-error-case)**
Per the new arm (d), the AC-16 exemption fires "only when arm (c)...is what would otherwise govern," and per AC-11c arm (c) *does* govern whenever pnpm-workspace.yaml is malformed. So a hybrid-root package.json sitting next to a broken pnpm-workspace.yaml must still get its root entry — but no criterion or AC-13 fixture proves it; AC-16's own fixture explicitly says "no pnpm-workspace.yaml anywhere." Suggested fix: add `AC-16b` (is_error_case: true) with the exact combined fixture (hybrid-root package.json + malformed pnpm-workspace.yaml → root entry still returned via arm c/d), and extend AC-13's group.

**2. AC-10 was never updated to carve out AC-16, unlike its own sibling AC-10c (ambiguous / internal contradiction)**
AC-10c already anticipated exactly this interaction on the Cargo side: "returns zero Cargo-ecosystem entries **besides any AC-07-style hybrid root package**." AC-10 (its npm mirror) says only "returns zero Npm-ecosystem entries" — no exception. AC-10's fixture never states the root package.json lacks a "name" field, so a root satisfying both AC-10 (`"workspaces": []`) and AC-16 (`"name"` + `"workspaces"` present) is a literal contradiction: AC-10 mandates zero entries, arm (d) mandates the root entry always survive. Suggested fix: rewrite AC-10's criterion text to add the same "besides any AC-16-style hybrid root package" carve-out AC-10c already has, and add `AC-10d` as a concrete proving fixture (root has both `"name"` and `"workspaces": []` → exactly one entry, path `"."`).

**3. The deferred pnpm-hybrid-root non_goals entry is a judgment call I'm overriding: it should be fixed now, not deferred (incomplete)**
The non_goals entry's reasoning — "pnpm-workspace.yaml carries no name/hybrid marker" — is true but irrelevant. AC-07/AC-16's actual principle is "the root directory's own package identity is never dropped by its own governing membership list," not "the identity field and the governing list must live in the same file." npm's root identity always lives in `package.json`'s `"name"` field regardless of which file governs membership (its own `"workspaces"` array, or a sibling `pnpm-workspace.yaml`). A pnpm monorepo with a named root `package.json` and a separate `pnpm-workspace.yaml` — an extremely common, arguably the *most* common pnpm layout — will silently lose its root package under the current text, which is externally indistinguishable from the bug AC-07/AC-16 exist to close. This is exactly the kind of "plausible-sounding, quietly-deferred" language this audit exists to catch. Suggested fix: delete the deferring non_goals entry; add `AC-17` (is_error_case: false) proving the pnpm-governed hybrid-root case; broaden api_surface arm (d) to `"the root package.json declares a 'name' field and dir is the workspace root itself, regardless of whether arm (b) or arm (c) governs the rest of npm membership filtering... (see AC-07, AC-16, AC-17)"`; and give `AC-10b` the same "besides any AC-17-style hybrid root package" carve-out AC-10 needs per finding 2.

## Not flagged
AC-16 itself and arm (d) as scoped are internally consistent, correctly cross-reference AC-08's precedence rule, and are grounded against the real `to_workspace_relative` behavior. AC-13's fixture/criterion-group lists for AC-16 are accurate. No untestable/vague language, no scope overlap with SPEC-TRACK3B or the flagged sibling specs, no new TBDs. The other 29 pre-existing criteria show no amendment-induced drift beyond the AC-10/AC-10b gaps above.

AC-16's own fixture explicitly excludes any pnpm-workspace.yaml, so the combined case (hybrid-root package.json + broken sibling pnpm-workspace.yaml) has no proving criterion, leaving room for an implementer to gate the exemption on 'no pnpm-workspace.yaml file at all' instead of 'no arm(b)-qualifying pnpm-workspace.yaml' — silently breaking real repos with an incomplete pnpm-workspace.yaml.",
